18265:    Deliberately NOT a hook. There are twenty-six of these sheets, written inline at twenty-six call
18266:    sites over the app's life; a hook would have to be added to twenty-six components and would put
18267:    the rules-of-hooks in the way of the ones rendered inside conditionals and callbacks. A ref
18268:    callback attaches to the element itself and can be dropped in anywhere.

18270:    HOW IT CLOSES. It does not take an onClose. Every one of these sheets is a child of a backdrop
18271:    that already closes it on click, and the sheet stops that click propagating — so the dismissal
18272:    the app already has is one synthetic click on the parent away. That means this works on all of
18273:    them without twenty-six different closers being threaded through, and a sheet whose backdrop is
18274:    deliberately inert simply springs back, which is the correct behaviour there anyway.

18277:    over a scrollable list belongs to that list. Both are what makes the gesture feel like part of

19287: /* THE PLAN — the whole block, read top to bottom.
19288:  *
19289:  * The first version laid weeks × days out as a GRID. It was wrong twice over. A six-day program made
19290:  * the grid wider than a phone, so it lived in a horizontal scroller — and a horizontal scroller
19291:  * without `touch-action: pan-x pan-y` swallows the vertical drag, so the page under it would not
19292:  * scroll at all. This file already had the fix for that in `.wpb-hscroll`; the grid simply did not
19293:  * use it. The layout was also the wrong shape for the question: a lifter reads a plan as a sequence
19294:  * of weeks, not as a matrix, and every cell had to be tiny to fit.
19295:  *

19297:  * Nothing scrolls sideways except the phase strip, which is short and uses .wpb-hscroll.
19298:  *
19299:  * READ-ONLY on purpose. Editing lives in ProgramView; a screen that both surveys and edits invites
19300:  * changing a week you are only looking at. Tapping a day inside a week opens it at that week. */
19301: /* A PHASE SHOULD LOOK LIKE WHAT IT ASKS OF YOU — IN THIS THEME, NOT IN SPITE OF IT.
19302:  *
19303:  * The first version hardcoded five hexes (a green, an amber, an orange, a violet, a blue). They read
19304:  * well against one palette and CLASHED with the rest, which is the predictable cost of writing fixed
19305:  * colours into an app that ships fourteen themes and lets the accent be anything.
19306:  *
19307:  * So the tones are DERIVED from the theme's own accent: same saturation, same lightness, hue rotated
19308:  * a little per phase. That keeps every phase unmistakably a member of the current palette while
19309:  * still making them distinguishable from each other, and it cannot clash — there is nothing to
19310:  * clash with, since every tone is the accent wearing a different coat. A deload rotates furthest and
19311:  * drops its saturation, because it is the phase that asks for less and should look like it.

21137: /* ═══ WEEKLY SCHEDULE SHEET ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
21138:  * Extracted from ProgramView at v632. The schedule is a real generation input, not decoration:
21139:  * `plannedGapHours` reads it (v587) and rotation follows it (v578), so a mis-assigned weekday
21140:  * shows up later as sets trimmed for "training ahead of plan". Props are the contract; the tap
21141:  * behaviour (rest → day 1 → … → last day → rest) stays with the owner as `onCycle`, because it
21142:  * is a setProgram reducer and this component owns no state. No hooks inside. */
21143: function WeeklyScheduleSheet({ program, onCycle, onClear, onClose }) {
21144:     const WD = ["Mon", "Tue", "Wed", "Thu", "Fri", "Sat", "Sun"];
21145:     const idx2wd = [1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 0]; // map display order to JS weekday (0=Sun)
21146:     const sched = program.schedule || {};
21147:     const trainingDays = idx2wd.filter(wd => sched[wd]).length;
21148:     const todayWd = new Date().getDay();
21149:     return (_jsx("div", { "data-testid": "weekly-schedule-sheet", onClick: () => onClose(), className: "wpb-backdrop", style: { position: "fixed", inset: 0, background: "rgba(0,0,0,.6)", display: "flex", alignItems: "flex-end", zIndex: 60, animation: "fadeIn .2s both" }, children: _jsxs("div", { ref: sheetDragRef, onClick: e => e.stopPropagation(), className: "wpb-scroll", style: { width: "100%", background: C.bg2, borderTopLeftRadius: 24, borderTopRightRadius: 24, padding: "10px 18px calc(env(safe-area-inset-bottom) + 26px)", animation: "sheetUp .28s cubic-bezier(.2,.7,.3,1) both", borderTop: `1px solid ${C.border}`, maxHeight: "86%", overflowY: "auto" }, children: [_jsxs("div", { style: { display: "flex", alignItems: "center", justifyContent: "space-between", marginBottom: 4 }, children: [_jsx("div", { style: { fontSize: 18, fontWeight: 700 }, children: "Weekly schedule" }), _jsx("button", { "aria-label": "Close", className: "pressable hit wpb-sheet-close", onClick: () => onClose(), style: iconBtn(), children: _jsx(X, { size: 18 }) })] }), _jsxs("div", { style: { fontSize: 13, color: C.muted, marginBottom: 16 }, children: ["Tap a day to assign a workout \u2014 ", trainingDays, " training ", trainingDays === 1 ? "day" : "days", ", ", 7 - trainingDays, " rest. Cycles through your days, then rest."] }), WD.map((label, i) => {
21150:                     const wd = idx2wd[i];
21151:                     const dayId = sched[wd];
21152:                     const day = dayId ? program.days.find(d => d.id === dayId) : null;
21153:                     const isToday = wd === todayWd;
21154:                     return (_jsxs("button", { "aria-label": `Schedule ${label}`, onClick: () => onCycle(wd), className: "pressable", style: { width: "100%", display: "flex", alignItems: "center", gap: 12, padding: "12px 14px", marginBottom: 6, borderRadius: 12, cursor: "pointer", textAlign: "left", background: day ? C.accentDim : C.card, border: `1px solid ${isToday ? C.accent : day ? C.accent + "44" : C.border}` }, children: [_jsxs("div", { style: { width: 42, flexShrink: 0 }, children: [_jsx("span", { style: { fontSize: 13, fontWeight: 700, color: day ? C.accent : C.muted }, children: label }), isToday && _jsx("div", { style: { fontSize: 11, fontWeight: 700, color: C.accentInk, textTransform: "uppercase", letterSpacing: .5 }, children: "Today" })] }), _jsx("div", { style: { flex: 1, minWidth: 0 }, children: day ? _jsx("span", { style: { fontSize: 15, fontWeight: 600, color: C.text }, children: day.label })
21155:                                     : _jsx("span", { style: { fontSize: 15, color: C.faint }, children: "Rest day" }) }), day ? _jsx(Dumbbell, { size: 15, color: C.accentInk, style: { flexShrink: 0 } }) : _jsx("span", { style: { fontSize: 11, color: C.faint, flexShrink: 0 }, children: "tap to set" })] }, wd));
21156:                 }), Object.keys(sched).length > 0 && (_jsx("button", { "aria-label": "Clear schedule", onClick: () => onClear(), className: "pressable", style: { width: "100%", marginTop: 6, padding: "11px", borderRadius: 12, border: `1px solid ${C.border}`, background: "none", color: C.muted, fontSize: 13, fontWeight: 600, cursor: "pointer" }, children: "Clear schedule" }))] }) }));

27314: /* estimated 1RM (Epley) lets us compare progress across different rep counts */
27315: // Estimated 1RM (Epley). The formula is only validated to ~10-12 reps; past that it inflates badly
27316: // (a 135×30 set would read as a 270 "1RM"). We cap the rep term at 12 so high-rep/endurance sets
27317: // don't produce fictitious maxes that would pollute PRs, strength scores and progression targets.
27318: /* A session's estimated 1RM must come from the BEST set actually performed — max over the logged
27319:    sets — not from perf.weight/perf.reps. That top-line summary is produced by summarizeSets(), which
27320:    deliberately records the FEWEST reps at the top weight ("the load you held for every set"): the
27321:    right anchor for progression, but flatly wrong as a 1RM estimate. Using it meant a session of
27322:    215×6,5,5,5,6 was scored off the 5 (251), while an older RAMPED session whose top tier held a
27323:    single hard set (205×8) got full credit (260) — so a lighter, older workout outranked a heavier,
27324:    stronger recent one, and "best e1RM" pointed at the wrong session entirely.
27325:    Legacy logs with no per-set data fall back to the summary, which is all they have. */
27326: /* ONE OWNER for the week bucket: the Monday 00:00 local timestamp a date falls in. Two identical
27327:    copies of this had grown, in analytics and in the progress views, and a date rule that forks is a
27328:    rule that will eventually disagree about which week a session belongs to on exactly one boundary. */

29978: // Weekly-average bodyweight trend. Single weigh-ins are noisy — water, sodium, digestion, and sleep
29979: // can swing the number a kg or more day to day, so comparing "today" against "the first-ever reading"
29980: // (as a raw before/after delta does) can easily read as a stall or a spike that isn't real. Bucketing
29981: // into rolling 7-day windows and comparing WEEKLY AVERAGES smooths that noise into an actual trend.
29982: // Buckets count backward from the most recent entry (not the calendar week), so it works the same
29983: // regardless of which day of the week someone happens to be looking at their trend on, and it's a
29984: // pure function of the log itself rather than depending on the wall-clock "now".
29985: // Returns null when there isn't enough spread to say anything (all entries inside one 7-day window).
29986: /* Every weigh-in records the unit it was taken in, exactly as history sessions do — but until now
29987:    nothing READ that tag. Switching units in Settings converts the whole log, so a single device
29988:    stayed consistent; a MERGE does not, because mergeStores unions two devices' logs by timestamp and
29989:    a phone on lb next to a tablet on kg produces one log holding both. Averaging those raw numbers
29990:    turned a steady 180 lb into a "loss" of 98 units a week. Normalising on read is the fix, and it
29991:    also covers entries written before the tag existed, which are read in the store's own unit. */
29992: function normalizeBwLog(bwLog, to) {
29993:     return (bwLog || [])
29994:         .filter(e => e && e.date != null && Number(e.w) > 0)
29995:         .map(e => ({ ...e, w: e.unit && to && e.unit !== to ? Math.round(toUnit(Number(e.w), e.unit, to) * 10) / 10 : Number(e.w), unit: to || e.unit }))
29996:         .sort((a, b) => a.date - b.date);
29997: }
29998: /* Month buckets for the session list, newest first, derived from the dates already on each entry.
29999:    Keyed by year-month rather than by label so December 2025 and December 2026 can never merge — a
30000:    label-keyed grouping looks correct for eleven months and then silently collapses two years of
30001:    training into one heading. The current year is left off the label because it is the common case
30002:    and repeating it on every header is noise; older years keep it, because that is when it matters. */

33020: function GymsSheet({ gyms, activeGymId, onSave, onClose }) {
33021:     const [list, setList] = useState(() => gyms.map(g => ({ ...g, equipment: [...g.equipment] })));

33023:     const [openId, setOpenId] = useState(null);
33024:     const [eqQuery, setEqQuery] = useState("");
33025:     const [tombed, setTombed] = useState({});
33026:     /* Every gym edit carries a timestamp. Without one the cross-device merge had nothing to compare
33027:        and gyms rode along on whichever device saved last — add a gym on your phone, sync with your
33028:        laptop, and it was gone from both. */
33029:     const patch = (id, fields) => setList(l => l.map(g => (g.id === id ? { ...g, ...fields, updatedAt: Date.now() } : g)));
33030:     const toggleEq = (g, eq) => patch(g.id, { equipment: g.equipment.includes(eq) ? g.equipment.filter(x => x !== eq) : [...g.equipment, eq] });
33031:     const addGym = (preset) => {
33032:         const id = newGymId();
33033:         // Name collisions are confusing in a switcher, so a second "Home gym" becomes "Home gym 2".
33034:         let name = preset.name, n = 2;
33035:         while (list.some(g => g.name === name))
33036:             name = `${preset.name} ${n++}`;
33037:         setList(l => [...l, { id, name, equipment: [...preset.equipment], updatedAt: Date.now() }]);
